## Words used in this dispatch, explained first

- **The derivation** — `cowork_blind_derivation_l2_2026_09_27.md` at the repository root: 49 statements
  L2-S1 to L2-S49 in six fields (Statement; *Defense*; *Source class*; *Status*; *Premise* with its
  false-negative path; *Falsifier*), and 18 open questions OQ-L2-1 to OQ-L2-18 as bullets in its §4, written
  by an implementation-blind session. It is a dated record of that act; under D-674's branch one its body is
  never rewritten.
- **The successor** — the new file Task 1 writes, `cowork_derived_specification_l2_2026_10_10.md` at the
  repository root (name authored here, overrulable by the user before Task 0): the derivation's §0 to §4
  carried whole with every amendment in place, each citing the record and ruling that made it; §5 to §7
  replaced by one pointer paragraph. **The derived L2 specification as amended, NOT RATIFIED.**
- **The reading file** — `ratification_surfaces/cowork_comparison_l2_reading.md`. **A row of §6** — one
  outgoing statement with its location, its derived statements, its current-text axis and its
  `*PROPOSED DISPOSITION.*` paragraph. A row split into claims is cited `Row m.n(i)`.
- **A ruled line** — ONE line ADDED immediately beneath a row's `*PROPOSED DISPOSITION.*` paragraph,
  opening `*RULED DISPOSITION (` and naming the record file and ruling, then the disposition in §5's
  closed vocabulary and the placement in the ruling's words. The proposed paragraph is never edited (#12).
- **A recorded line** — the same, opening `*RECORDED DISPOSITION (` — used ONLY where a record states that
  a row's placement follows from the record as this side's derivation and was NOT ruled (the records say
  "recorded as derivation, not asked" or "follows from the record"). It names the record and section and
  says *challengeable at the row*. **Its form is form (d) below, fixed at its first use (§L.12).**
- **An amendment** — a change a record orders to a derived statement, an open question or the terms: a
  status change, a clause written beside a statement, a falsifier clause replaced, an open question answered.
- **The ledger** — §L below: per record, the writing side's transcription of what each ruling orders, as a
  locator aid. **The record's own words govern; where the ledger and the record differ, the record governs
  and the difference is reported.**
- **A changed passage** — a run of lines a diff shows as changed together; git's own diff output calls it a
  *hunk* (the stretch under one `@@ … @@` line). Where a task names its changed passages by their SITES (Task 5's
  three sites in the L0/L1 specification, S-50's site holding three insertions that lie apart), git may show one
  site as more than one hunk: count by site, as the task names them. *Added 2026-10-13 at the whole-draft pass,
  the sitting booted on entry 372, under the writing side's vocabulary rule — "a changed passage for hunk", one
  of the earlier vocabulary rulings the 2026-08-17 eighth-return record's §3 names
  (`records/cowork/rulings/cowork_rulings_2026_08_17_eighth_return.md`, read there by this sitting). The word
  "hunk" formerly stood where this dispatch now writes "changed passage" at these sites, and nothing else of
  those sentences changed: A5 ("ONE hunk"); Task 1(e) ("Every hunk", "A hunk none of those") and E1 ("every hunk
  attributed"); Task 2's check ("exactly ONE hunk") and E2 ("one hunk"); Task 3(f) ("every hunk", "a hunk of
  any other kind") and E3 ("attributed hunk by hunk", now "attributed passage by passage"); Task 4's proof
  ("exactly ONE hunk", "in no hunk") and E4 ("one hunk"); Task 5's proof ("in no hunk", twice); Task 6 item 2
  ("the per-hunk attribution tables", now "the per-passage attribution tables").*

## The forms, fixed here so the batch takes no decision

**(a) Amendments in the successor** (the L0/L1 fifth dispatch's Task 1(c), adapted):
- A **status change**: the *Status* field is edited; the former value preserved in the same field as
  `(was: "<former>"; <record file>, Ruling N)`.
- A **falsifier clause replaced**: the *Falsifier* field edited, the former clause preserved in the same
  field as `(was: "<former clause>"; <record file>, Ruling N)`.
- A **clause beside a statement**: a sub-item beneath the six fields, `- *Beside L2-Sn (<record file>,
  Ruling N).*` + the record's words quoted.
- A statement that **stands as derived** by a ruling's words: a sub-item `- *Ruled (<record file>, Ruling
  N).* Stands as derived.` — no text change.
- An open question **answered**: it stays in §4, the bullet gaining `**ANSWERED (<record file>, Ruling N):**`
  and the record's answering sentence quoted beneath it.
- A statement that **stands as derived** by a record's OWN derivation and NOT by a ruling (a record section
  headed as not a ruling, "the record decides them", "recorded as this side's derivation (D-599)"): a
  sub-item `- *Recorded (<record file>, §<section>).* Stands as derived — recorded by the record as this
  side's derivation, not ruled; challengeable.` + the record's words quoted — no text change. *Authored by the
  writing side at its first use (§L.13, L2-S43), on the shape of form (d) below; the user may overrule it
  before Task 0.*
- A **Defense clause replaced** (a record names a statement's *Defense* as what a ruling amends, with the
  wording not ruled): the *Defense* field edited at that clause only, the former clause preserved in the same
  field as `(was: "<former clause>"; <record file>, Ruling N)`, on the shape of the falsifier-clause form
  above. *Authored by the writing side at its first use (§L.15, L2-S40); the user may overrule it before
  Task 0.*
- **No statement is renumbered and none is added as a new L2-S number.**

**(b) Lines in the reading file:**
`*RULED DISPOSITION (<record file>, Ruling N).* **<disposition>** — <placement in the record's words>.`
A row the record places "as proposed" still gets its line, saying *as proposed*. **A row named by two
records gets both records' lines, in ledger order, and is listed in the report under *rows named by more
than one record*; where the two lines' dispositions differ, that row is a STOP-and-report and gets
neither.** Where a record's placement
of a split row names only one claim, the line names that claim: `(i) **…**`.

**(c) A flagged item answered** (a §6 row that carries no `*PROPOSED DISPOSITION.*` paragraph, only the
line `*Not placed, as the L0/L1 file states of it.*`, and that a record says it answers): ONE line ADDED
immediately beneath that line, `*RULED (<record file>, Ruling N).* Answered — <the record's sentence>.` No
disposition word is written, and the row is not counted in the ruled distribution. *This form is authored
by the writing side at its first use (§L.10, Row 62.55); the user may overrule it before Task 0.*

**(d) A recorded line** (a row a record says takes its placement as this side's derivation, NOT as a
ruling): ONE line ADDED immediately beneath the row's `*PROPOSED DISPOSITION.*` paragraph,
`*RECORDED DISPOSITION (<record file>, §<section>).* **<disposition>** — <as proposed, or the placement in
the record's words>; <the record's ground for the row, in its words>. Recorded by the record as this side's
derivation, not ruled; challengeable at the row.` The proposed paragraph is never edited (#12). In the
ruled distribution (Task 3), rows carrying a recorded line are counted in a column of their own, never
added into the ruled counts. A row that two records name, one by a ruled line and one by a recorded line, follows form (b)'s
two-record rule: both lines, listed in the report, and a STOP-and-report if their dispositions differ.
*Fixed
here at its first use (§L.12, the tractability record's §1c); the user may overrule it before Task 0.*

## The standing bars, and the commit-and-push discipline

**The standing bars bind this batch whole:** no `src/` edit, no golden, no test changed, moved or run,
nothing under `tools/corpus/` or `tools/robust_stop/`, **no measurement of the ANALYSIS built, designed,
scoped or run**, no design, no repair, **no derivation of any specification statement — an amendment is
the record's words placed, never this batch's**, NO SESSION BOOTED, no document archived, moved or deleted
AS A FILE, **no open-items row created, flipped or discarded, except an apparatus-finding row under rule (c)**
*(aligned 2026-10-13 by the sitting booted on entry 373 with the list* What this batch does NOT do *and with
`CLAUDE.md`'s rule (c); the user was told on the stale-field surface and did not object; former wording: "no
open-items row created, flipped or discarded")*, **no edit to any governing document (the
`STATUS.md`/`STATUS_ARCHIVE.md` acts of Task 6, the `CLAUDE.md` edits Task 4 orders — the step-4 clause and
two sentences of the same block's closing paragraph — and the three
sites of `cowork_derived_specification_l0_l1_2026_09_03.md` and the one list of `FRAMEWORK.md` Task 5 orders
excepted)** *(widened 2026-10-13 by the sitting booted on entry 373 when Task 4 gained the closing paragraph's two
sentences; former wording: "the one clause of `CLAUDE.md` Task 4 orders")* *(corrected 2026-10-13 at the check of the Tasks, the sitting booted on entry 371: the exception
named Tasks 6 and 4 and not Task 5, whose two files A5 already excepts; former wording: "(the
`STATUS.md`/`STATUS_ARCHIVE.md` acts of Task 6 and the one clause of `CLAUDE.md` Task 4 orders excepted)")*, to
the brief, to the pack or its generator, to any register entry or register source, to the suspension file,
to any outgoing text of the comparison, or to the derivation's BODY** — the derivation gains one top banner
and nothing else — **no ruling taken, no position weighed, no verdict on the derivation, on any open
question, or on the deriving session's independence**, no re-grading of any current-text axis, **no
recommendation anywhere** — not in the successor, not in the reading file, not in the report, not in a
commit message. The points the ledger notes *for the user before Task 0* and *for the check turn* are
NOT yours: where the user has not ruled on one before Task 0, the batch applies the draft as written.

**Commit-and-push per task. Run the FULL guard set BEFORE the first edit (`--check`) and again at the end
(write mode, committed as the artifact of a real run). Verify every commit at the object by explicit hash.
Every shell command carries `; echo "exit:$?"`. `git status` and `git diff` over the working tree time out
on this mount and are denied by the armed guard — use the per-path explicit-hash forms named in A1; a `git
diff` with NO explicit hash is a D-253 breach even where the guard admits it. If staging is refused by a
stale zero-length `index.lock` with no git process running, establish the cause (D-669), remove it, report
the act. Plain single-quoted `git commit -m '…'` subjects. The reserved-word and vocabulary rules bind every
line — *score* only the musical score, *key* only tonality, *bar* only the metric unit (the prohibiting
sense is *exclude*), no numeric grade anywhere; the disposition vocabulary is the reading file's §5 closed
set and nothing else, with the two qualifiers the ledger carries as the records' own (*in part*; *as far as
they reach L2* / *for L2*) and the one stretched destination §L.43 carries in the record's own words.**

`records/cowork/handoff/cowork_handoff.md` and entry 375 say it stands — read both.** *(Former wording:
"entry 362"; brought up 2026-10-13 at the banner's removal, the sitting booted on entry 374.)* **A row a record
names whose content, read at the row, does not match what the record says of it** (a row number that names
a different outgoing statement than the record describes; a record placing a row under a disposition the
reading file's §5 does not carry, the two qualifiers and the stretched destination above excepted; a row
two records would place differently) **is a STOP-and-report for THAT ROW: the row is left at its proposed
disposition, listed in the report under *rows the act could not place* with what was read, and the rest of
the record is applied.** An amendment whose target statement does not carry the wording the ledger quotes
as standing at the derivation is a STOP-and-report for that amendment, the rest applied. Nothing is
repaired, re-read into a different placement, or placed by this batch's own judgment.

### §L.32 — `records/cowork/rulings/cowork_rulings_2026_10_07_context_research_sitting.md`

One ruling, on the order of work: **Ruling 1** — one research iteration over all layers, *"after the last layer's detail
specification is ratified and before the measurement-design phase opens"*, asking per statement *"is there a published
method, algorithm or public software that would infer better than what is specified?"*, aimed at the load-bearing
statements and read under D-473 (user: *"put this research-iteration in the plan as you suggested, then carary on."*,
quoted at the record verbatim). The record's head: *"It files a plan only: it changes no specification and no algorithm
as any specification defines it."* Its *"What it does not do"*: *"It changes no specification, no statement of any layer's
algorithm, no phase definition and no ruling; it adds a step to the order of work."* The record places no
row, amends no derived statement and answers no open question of the derivation (*searched at the record by the writing
side:* "L2-S" occurs twice, each time in "L2-S22" naming the targeted reading of the same sitting; "OQ-" and "Row" occur
nowhere). The record was read whole by the writing side.

**Successor:** no amendment. **Rows:** none placed, so form (b)'s two-record rule fires nowhere from it. **Transfer
list:** nothing. *Not applied here, per the record:* no specification, statement, phase definition or ruling is changed
(its *"What it does not do"*); no decisions-register identity is allocated (its head, the register's rule (c) being
suspended). It is landed at Task 0 with the other records of 2026-10-04 to 2026-10-10, as the frame already provides.
